LD2704 153795 Tallinn Estonian Television Network in
Estonian 1800 GMT 26 Apr 95

[FBIS Translated Text] For decades the Council of
Europe has supported Estonia’s independence and has
de jure recognized the existence of our state. This is what
President Lennart Meri said on his return from Stras-
bourg, from a plenary session of the Council of Europe's
parliamentary assembly. The first Estonian president to
speak at a plenary session, Meri stated that Estonia has
an honest and just nationality policy, and our liberal
citizenship law serves as its cornerstone.

At a meeting with Council of Europe Secretary-General
Daniel Tarschys and parliamentary assembly president
Miguel-Angel Martinez, relations between Estonia and
the Council of Europe were discussed as well as the
implementation in Estonia of Council of Europe pro-
grams worth up to 6 million Estonian kroons.

Touching on Estonian-Russian relations, Meri said that
the areas of land [which were formerly part of prewar
Estonia but} which are presently under Russian control
must be viewed separately from the Tartu Peace Treaty
[of 1920).

[Begin recording] [Meri] The Tartu Peace Treaty 1s the
treaty through which the Republic of Estonia was born.
This treaty acts as our birth certificate and will remain to
serve as the foundation to our statehood. If we have any
territorial disputes with the Russian Federation they
must be resolved before we step closer to the European
structures because joining the European structures
means the absence of any territonal disputes. [Meri
pauses] I expect that I have now answered your question

{Unidentified correspondent] How realistic 1s it to have
a border agreement concluded with Russia this year?

[Meri] During the past year Estoma has constantly
highlighted its readiness to deal with these matters. The
Russian Federation has postponed them. | would expect
that after the elections Russia will find it more favorable
to handle them. At present it seems that Russia’s attitude
is largely influenced by the upcoming Duma elections,
perhaps also by the presidential elections which will spill
into next year, so that it 1s very difficult to speculate
here. But we have the support of very many countries as
well as of international organizations for theses issues to
be resolved as soon as possible. This, | am convinced, 1s
in Estonia's interests, in the interests of Europe, northern
Europe, and in the interests of European security. [end
recording}

and his predecessor Kaido Kama

of the Defense Forces General Staff. He was the man
appointed to command the rebellious “jaager” company

in the summer of 1993. After the company was dis-

and guns in their possession voluntarily and without

Trade Deficit Hits Record High

W'S2804102495 Tallinn BNS in English 1530 GMT 27
ipr 95

{[FBIS Transcribed Text] Tallinn, Apr 27, BNS—The
deficit of Estonia’s foreign trade in March was 704.2
million kroons, breaking the previous record of 665.3
million kroons reported for December last year.

Estonia exported 1,819.0 million kroons worth of goods
and commodities in March, while imports totaled
2,523.2 million kroons. Exports in March were up by
232.0 millon kroons from the previous month, with
imports skyrocketing up by 511.9 million kroons over
the same period.

In the first quarter this year, Estonia’s exports totaled
5,045.3 million kroons, and imports, 6,564.5 million
kroons. Imports exceeded exports by 1,159.6 million
kroons in the January-March quarter.

First quarter's export was up 28 percent and import 27
percent from the respective three-month period a vear
ago

Estonia traded in March with partners in 98 countries
and had a positive balance with 53 of them.

Saeima Speaker on Talks With European
Parliament

LD2604 142498

[FBIS Editonal Report] Riga Radio Riga Network in
Latvian at 0901 GMT on 26 April carries a 25 April

interview with Saeima speaker Anatolijs Gorbunovs by
correspondent Ainars Asaks on an interparliamentary
meeting of the Council of Europe in Brussels. The
speaker began with the following.

The speaker says the following:

“It was very important that the MPs from commissions
participated there with their reports—from European
Parliament commissions—they were with specific
reports. And I was also pleased that we really represented
the Latvian Saeima—its political spectrum, all the fac-
tions were represented.”

Asked about how the Latvian delegation, which was
taking part in such an interparliamentary meeting for the
first time, was received, Gorbunovs says:

“Talks also touched upon the issues of Latvian security,
i.e. external and internal security. And priorities which
we are discussing here in Latvia, they also were men-
tioned there, in the European Parliament—that this—
also a political dialogue on good neighborly relations and
cooperation has to be continued in the Baltic Sea region,
rather than with such confrontational elements which
unfortunately exist and are voiced by the Russian side,
which are voiced in our direction. Of course, appropri-
ately assessing such statements, possibilities for cooper-
ation in the Baltic region have to be put—and I think
these priorities sounded well enough.

“Also perhaps about our internal security, that 1s about
internal stability. And the fact that the factions were
expressing different opinions, because they see this
internal stability differently, that was also understand-
able to them and perhaps it was even good that they
heard these different opinions and also the laws which
we have passed—that is the vote of the majority of the
Saeima.”

The correspondent asks whether some kind of a resume
was adopted on this meeting. Gorbunovs says

“They set up a special delegation for cooperation with
the Baltic states. And the most important resume ts that
in practice we have already begun a dialogue which they
cleverly call a structured dialogue. And the dialogue has

started and it will continue and I think rather soon, of

BALTIC STATES 85

Gheorghe Frunda visited Vilnius in the middle of March
on behalf of the above committee in order to examine
how Lithuania is observing its commitments to the
European Union which it undertook when entering the
Council of Europe. George Frunda was received by
Lithuanian President Algirdas Brazauskas and by
Seimas Chairman Ceslovas Jursenas. He also met mem-
bers of Seimas committees and factions, officials of the
Supreme Court and of the Constitutional Court, heads of
the Ministry of Justice, leaders of the religious commu-
nities, and representatives of the national minorities.
Gheorghe Frunda also visited Lithuanian prisons.

The official report by Gheorghe Frunda will be presented
this summer at a regular session of the assembly. Prelim-
inary conclusions, meanwhile, were presented by Gheo-
rghe Frunda at the committee session. These conclusions
were endorsed this week.

According to Kazys Bobelis, some statements made by
Gheorghe Frunda imply that human rights are flagrantly
violated in Lithuania. The Lithuanian delegation cannot
agree with such a biased assessment of the situation in
Lithuania, Kazys Bobelis said.

Gheorghe Frunda said that over the past three years
Lithuania has not taken a single step to implement its
commitments to the Council of Europe. Responding to
this allegation, Kazys Bobelis reminded Gheorghe
Frunda that Lith tania became member of the Council of
Europe on 14 May 1993. Lithuania committed itself to
observing international conventions and it is observing
them, Bobelis said.

Meanwhile, according to Gheorghe Frunda, the general
prosecutor's office of Lithuania continues to operate in
the Soviet manner. Arrested persons do not have the
possibility to consult a lawyer before the court hearings
and they are not allowed to meet their relatives. Gheo-
rghe Frunda noted that persons awaiting the death
penalty are being kept in especially bad conditions. In his
report, Frunda criticized the procedure for appointing
attorneys in Lithuania.

Gheorghe Frunda also said that the rights of the believers
among the national minorities are violated in Lithuania.

Kazys Bobelis told the news conference that the Lithua-
nian delegation will issue an official statement as soon as
Frunda’s report is officially presented to the Council of
Europe. We will reject all accusations as totally
unfounded, Bobelis stressed.

At the same time, Kazys Bobelis noted that conclusions
presented in another report by Gheorghe Frunda are
unbiased and favorable to Lithuania.

Accord Scheduled for Associate EU Membership

{[FBIS Transcribed Text] VILNIUS, April 27 (Inter-
fax}—On May 29 Lithuania will sign an agreement on

86 BALTIC STATES

associated membership with the European Union, For-
eign Minister Povilas Gylys said on the national radio on
Thursday with reference to preliminary information.

He described the upcoming event as a momentous
historic occasion, an important step on the road of
Lithuania’s rapprochement with the European Union.

Lithuania will follow an even more active policy of
integration into European structures, Gylys pointed out.

According to him, Lithuania’s government is draft: .g a
resolution on forming in its Foreign Ministry a European
department, which is expected to begin its work in June.
Its main function will be to develop comprehensive ties
with the European Union and its member states, coor-
dinate activity aimed at furthering trade relations and

FBIS-SOV-95-082
28 April 1995

technical aid, and bring Lithuanian laws regulating eco-
nomic and other activity into line with European stan-
dards.

Along with seeking to integrate into European political,
economic and defense structures, Lithuania will follow a
foreign policy designed to secure friendly relations with
the neighbor countries, the foreign minister said.

With reference to the recent statement by his Russian
counterpart Andrey Kozyrev as to the possibility of using
Russian armed forces to protect the rights of ethnic
Russians abroad, Gylys described it as “absolutely unac-
ceptable.” This attitude was officially expressed to the
Russian ambassador to Lithuania, who was requested to
give the necessary explanation, he said.
